href="/wiki/Gilbertese_language" title="Gilbertese language">Gilbertese</a>: <i lang="gil">[Ribaberiki] Kiribati</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-r1_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-r1-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> is an <a href="/wiki/Island_country" title="Island country">island country</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Micronesia" title="Micronesia">Micronesia</a> subregion of <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a> in the central <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">Pacific Ocean</a>. Its permanent population is over 119,000 as of the 2020 census, with more than half living on <a href="/wiki/Tarawa" title="Tarawa">Tarawa</a> atoll. The state comprises <a href="/wiki/List_of_islands_of_Kiribati" title="List of islands of Kiribati">32 atolls</a> and one remote <a href="/wiki/Raised_coral_atoll" title="Raised coral atoll">raised coral island</a>, <a href="/wiki/Banaba" title="Banaba">Banaba</a>. Its total land area is 811&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (313&#160;sq&#160;mi)<sup id="cite_ref-IMF_2011_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IMF_2011-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> dispersed over 3,441,810&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (1,328,890&#160;sq&#160;mi) of <a href="/wiki/Ocean" title="Ocean">ocean</a>.
</p><p>The islands' spread straddles the <a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">equator</a> and the <a href="/wiki/180th_meridian" title="180th meridian">180th meridian</a>, although the <a href="/wiki/International_Date_Line" title="International Date Line">International Date Line</a> goes around Kiribati and swings far to the east, almost reaching <a href="/wiki/150th_meridian_west" title="150th meridian west">150°W</a>. This brings Kiribati's easternmost islands, the southern <a href="/wiki/Line_Islands" title="Line Islands">Line Islands</a> south of Hawaii, into the same day as the <a href="/wiki/Gilbert_Islands" title="Gilbert Islands">Gilbert Islands</a> and places them in the most advanced time zone on Earth: <a href="/wiki/UTC%2B14:00" title="UTC+14:00">UTC+14</a>.
</p><p>Kiribati gained its independence from the United Kingdom, becoming a <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_state" title="Sovereign state">sovereign state</a> in 1979. The capital, <a href="/wiki/South_Tarawa" title="South Tarawa">South Tarawa</a>, now the most populated area, consists of a number of islets, connected by a series of <a href="/wiki/Causeway" title="Causeway">causeways</a>. These comprise about half the area of <a href="/wiki/Tarawa" title="Tarawa">Tarawa Atoll</a>. Prior to its independence, the country had exported <a href="/wiki/Phosphate" title="Phosphate">phosphate</a>, but those mines are no longer viable with <a href="/wiki/Fisheries" class="mw-redirect" title="Fisheries">fisheries</a> and export of <a href="/wiki/Copra" title="Copra">copra</a> driving much of the economy. Kiribati is one of the <a href="/wiki/Least_developed_countries" title="Least developed countries">least developed countries</a> in the world and is highly dependent on international aid for its economy.
</p><p>Kiribati is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Community" title="Pacific Community">Pacific Community</a>, <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>, the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a>, the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_of_African,_Caribbean_and_Pacific_States" title="Organisation of African, Caribbean and Pacific States">Organisation of African, Caribbean and Pacific States</a>, and became a full member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> in 1999.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:2_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:3_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> As an island nation, the islands are <a href="/wiki/Effects_of_climate_change_on_small_island_countries" title="Effects of climate change on small island countries">vulnerable to climate change</a> and tsunamis. <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_mitigation" title="Climate change mitigation">Addressing climate change</a> has been a central part of its international policy, as a member of the <a href="/wiki/Alliance_of_Small_Island_States" title="Alliance of Small Island States">Alliance of Small Island States</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology_and_pronunciation">Etymology and pronunciation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology and pronunciation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The name is pronounced <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/ɪr/: &#39;irr&#39; in &#39;mirror&#39;">ɪr</span><span title="/ɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;kit&#39;">ɪ</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span></span>/</a></span></span>, <i>Kiribass</i>, as <i>-ti</i> in the <a href="/wiki/Gilbertese_language" title="Gilbertese language">Gilbertese language</a> represents an <i><a href="/wiki/S" title="S">s</a></i> sound. Similarly, the name of its people, the I-Kiribati, is pronounced as <i>e-Kiribass</i>.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:FMIB_53006_Gilbert_or_Kingsmill_Islands.jpeg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/FMIB_53006_Gilbert_or_Kingsmill_Islands.jpeg/220px-FMIB_53006_Gilbert_or_Kingsmill_Islands.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="340" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/FMIB_53006_Gilbert_or_Kingsmill_Islands.jpeg/330px-FMIB_53006_Gilbert_or_Kingsmill_Islands.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/FMIB_53006_Gilbert_or_Kingsmill_Islands.jpeg/440px-FMIB_53006_Gilbert_or_Kingsmill_Islands.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="490" data-file-height="758" /></a><figcaption>Map of the Gilbert or Kingsmill Islands, 1890</figcaption></figure>
<p>The name "Kiribati" was adopted in 1979 at independence. It is the <a href="/wiki/Gilbertese" class="mw-redirect" title="Gilbertese">Gilbertese</a> rendition of Gilberts, the plural of the English name of the nation's main archipelago, the <a href="/wiki/Gilbert_Islands" title="Gilbert Islands">Gilbert Islands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> It was named <i>îles Gilbert</i> (French for <i>Gilbert Islands</i>) in about 1820 by Russian admiral <a href="/wiki/Adam_Johann_von_Krusenstern" title="Adam Johann von Krusenstern">Adam von Krusenstern</a><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> and French captain <a href="/wiki/Louis-Isidore_Duperrey" title="Louis-Isidore Duperrey">Louis Duperrey</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> after the British <a href="/wiki/Sea_captain" title="Sea captain">captain</a> <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Gilbert_(sea_captain)" title="Thomas Gilbert (sea captain)">Thomas Gilbert</a>. Gilbert and captain <a href="/wiki/John_Marshall_(Royal_Navy_officer)" class="mw-redirect" title="John Marshall (Royal Navy officer)">John Marshall</a> sighted some of the islands in 1788, while crossing the "outer passage" route from <a href="/wiki/Port_Jackson" title="Port Jackson">Port Jackson</a> to <a href="/wiki/Guangzhou" title="Guangzhou">Canton</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-jps.auckland.ac.nz_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jps.auckland.ac.nz-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMacdonald1982_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMacdonald1982-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-LifeMagazine1944-05-22_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LifeMagazine1944-05-22-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Both von Krusenstern's and Duperrey's maps, published in 1824, were written in French.
</p><p>In French, the Northern Islands were until then called <i>îles Mulgrave</i> and <a href="/wiki/Nikunau" title="Nikunau">Byron Island</a> was not part of them. In English, the archipelago, particularly the southern part, was often referred to as the Kingsmills in the 19th century, although the name Gilbert Islands was used increasingly, including in the <a href="/wiki/British_Western_Pacific_Territories" title="British Western Pacific Territories">Western Pacific Order in Council</a> of 1877 and in the Pacific Order of 1893.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The name Gilbert, already in the name of the British protectorate since 1892, was incorporated into the name of the entire <a href="/wiki/Gilbert_and_Ellice_Islands_Colony" class="mw-redirect" title="Gilbert and Ellice Islands Colony">Gilbert and Ellice Islands Colony</a> (GEIC) from 1916 and was retained after the Ellice Islands became the separate nation of <a href="/wiki/Tuvalu" title="Tuvalu">Tuvalu</a> in 1976. The spelling of Gilberts in the <a href="/wiki/Gilbertese_language" title="Gilbertese language">Gilbertese language</a> as Kiribati may be found in books in Gilbertese prepared by missionaries, but with the meaning of Gilbertese (<a href="/wiki/Demonym" title="Demonym">demonym</a> and language) (see e.g., Hawaiian Board of Missionaries, 1895).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHBM1895_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHBM1895-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The first mention as a dictionary entry of the word <i>Kiribati</i> as the native name of the country was written down in 1952 by <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ernest_Sabatier&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ernest Sabatier (page does not exist)">Ernest Sabatier</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernest_Sabatier" class="extiw" title="fr:Ernest Sabatier">fr</a>&#93;</span> in his comprehensive <i>Dictionnaire gilbertin-français</i>.
</p><p>The indigenous name often suggested for the Gilbert Islands proper is <i>Tungaru</i> (see e.g., <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ernest_Sabatier&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ernest Sabatier (page does not exist)">Ernest Sabatier</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernest_Sabatier" class="extiw" title="fr:Ernest Sabatier">fr</a>&#93;</span>, 1952–1953, or <a href="/wiki/Arthur_Grimble" title="Arthur Grimble">Arthur Grimble</a>, 1989<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGrimble1989_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrimble1989-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>). The rendition Kiribati for Gilberts was chosen as the official name of the new independent nation by the chief minister, Sir <a href="/wiki/Ieremia_Tabai" title="Ieremia Tabai">Ieremia Tabai</a> and his <a href="/wiki/Cabinet_of_Kiribati" title="Cabinet of Kiribati">cabinet</a>, on such grounds that it was modern,<sup id="cite_ref-cind_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cind-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> and to comprehend the inclusion of outer islands (e.g., <a href="/wiki/Phoenix_Islands" title="Phoenix Islands">the Phoenix Group</a> and Line Islands), which were not considered part of the Tungaru (or Gilberts) chain.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESabatier1954_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESabatier1954-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;note 1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto1_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Kiribati" title="History of Kiribati">History of Kiribati</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tabiteuean_warriors_of_the_Gilberts_Islands,_about_1840.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Tabiteuean_warriors_of_the_Gilberts_Islands%2C_about_1840.png/220px-Tabiteuean_warriors_of_the_Gilberts_Islands%2C_about_1840.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Tabiteuean_warriors_of_the_Gilberts_Islands%2C_about_1840.png/330px-Tabiteuean_warriors_of_the_Gilberts_Islands%2C_about_1840.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Tabiteuean_warriors_of_the_Gilberts_Islands%2C_about_1840.png/440px-Tabiteuean_warriors_of_the_Gilberts_Islands%2C_about_1840.png 2x" data-file-width="544" data-file-height="502" /></a><figcaption>Gilbertese warriors of <a href="/wiki/Tabiteuea" title="Tabiteuea">Tabiteuea</a>, with shark's teeth weapons, <i>circa</i> 1841</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_history">Early history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Early history">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The area now called Kiribati, mainly the 16 <a href="/wiki/Gilbert_Islands" title="Gilbert Islands">Gilbert Islands</a>, has been inhabited by <a href="/wiki/Austronesian_peoples" title="Austronesian peoples">Austronesian peoples</a> speaking the same <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_languages" title="Oceanic languages">Oceanic language</a>, from north to south, including the southernmost <a href="/wiki/Nui_(atoll)" title="Nui (atoll)">Nui</a>, since sometime between 3000 BCE<sup id="cite_ref-cind_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cind-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> and 1300 CE.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThomas20035_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas20035-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The area was not completely isolated; later, voyagers from <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tonga" title="Tonga">Tonga</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a> introduced some <a href="/wiki/Polynesia" title="Polynesia">Polynesian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Melanesia" title="Melanesia">Melanesian</a> cultural aspects, respectively. Intermarriage and intense navigation between the islands tended to blur cultural differences and resulted in a significant degree of cultural homogenization.<sup id="cite_ref-brit_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Local oral historians chiefly in the form of lore keepers suggest that the area was first inhabited by a group of seafaring people from <a href="/wiki/Melanesia" title="Melanesia">Melanesia</a>, who were described as being dark-skinned, frizzy-haired, and short in stature. These <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples" title="Indigenous peoples">indigenous peoples</a> were then visited by early <a href="/wiki/Austronesian_peoples" title="Austronesian peoples">Austronesian</a> seafarers from the west, a place called Matang, orally described as being tall and fair-skinned. Eventually, both groups intermittently clashed and intermingled until they slowly became a uniform population.
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Makin_Islander.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Makin_Islander.jpg/200px-Makin_Islander.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Makin_Islander.jpg/300px-Makin_Islander.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Makin_Islander.jpg/400px-Makin_Islander.jpg 2x" data-file-width="591" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>Portrait of a native of the <a href="/wiki/Makin_(islands)" class="mw-redirect" title="Makin (islands)">Makin</a> Islands, drawn by <a href="/wiki/Alfred_Thomas_Agate" title="Alfred Thomas Agate">Alfred Thomas Agate</a> (1841)</figcaption></figure>
<p>Around 1300 CE, a mass departure occurred from <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a> leading to the addition of Polynesian ancestry into the mix of most Gilbertese people. These Samoans later brought strong features of <a href="/wiki/Polynesian_languages" title="Polynesian languages">Polynesian languages</a> and culture, creating clans based on their own Samoan traditions and slowly intertwining with the indigenous clans and powers already dominant in Kiribati. Around the 15th century, starkly contrasting systems of governance arose between the northern islands, primarily under chiefly rule (<i>uea</i>), and the central and southern islands, primarily under the rule of their council of elders (<i>unimwaane</i>). <a href="/wiki/Tabiteuea" title="Tabiteuea">Tabiteuea</a> could be an exception as the sole island that is known as maintaining a traditional <a href="/wiki/Egalitarianism" title="Egalitarianism">egalitarian</a> society. The name Tabiteuea stems from the root phrase <i>Tabu-te-Uea</i>, meaning "chiefs are forbidden".<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Civil war soon became a factor, with acquisition of land being the main form of conquest. Clans and chiefs began fighting over resources, fueled by hatred and reignited blood feuds, which may have started months, years, or even decades before.
</p><p>The turmoil lasted well into the European visitation and colonial era, which led to certain islands decimating their foes with the help of guns and cannon-equipped ships that Europeans provided to some I-Kiribati leaders.<sup id="cite_ref-jps.auckland.ac.nz_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jps.auckland.ac.nz-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The typical military arms of the I-Kiribati at this time were shark tooth-embedded wooden spears, knives, and swords, and garbs of armour fashioned from dense coconut fibre. They chiefly used these instead of the gunpowder and weapons of steel available at the time, because of the strong sentimental value of the equipment handed down through generations. Ranged weapons, such as bows, slings, and javelins, were seldom used; <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Kiribati#Kiribati_traditional_martial_arts" title="Culture of Kiribati">hand-to-hand combat</a> was a prominent skill still practised today, though seldom mentioned because of various taboos associated with it, secrecy being the primary one. <a href="/wiki/Abemama" title="Abemama">Abemama</a>'s High Chief <a href="/wiki/Tembinok%27" title="Tembinok&#39;">Tembinok'</a> was the last of the dozens of expansionist chiefs of Gilbert Islands of this period, despite Abemama historically conforming to the traditional southern islands' governance of their respective <i>unimwaane</i>. He was immortalised in <a href="/wiki/Robert_Louis_Stevenson" title="Robert Louis Stevenson">Robert Louis Stevenson</a>'s book <i>In the South Seas</i>, which delved into the high chief's character and method of rule during Stevenson's stay in Abemama. The 90th anniversary of his arrival in the Gilbert Islands was chosen to celebrate the independence of Kiribati on 12 July 1979.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Colonial_era">Colonial era</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Colonial era">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/British_Western_Pacific_Territories" title="British Western Pacific Territories">British Western Pacific Territories</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gilbert_and_Ellice_Islands" title="Gilbert and Ellice Islands">Gilbert and Ellice Islands</a></div>
<p>Chance visits by European ships occurred in the 17th and 18th centuries,<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> while those ships attempted circumnavigations of the world, or sought sailing routes from the south to north Pacific Ocean. A passing trade, whaling the On-The-Line grounds,<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> and labour ships associated with <a href="/wiki/Blackbirding" title="Blackbirding">blackbirding</a> of <a href="/wiki/Kanaka_(Pacific_Island_worker)" title="Kanaka (Pacific Island worker)">Kanakas</a> workers, visited the islands in large numbers during the 19th century, with social, economic, political, religious and cultural consequences. More than 9,000 workers were sent abroad from 1845 to 1895, most of them not returning.<sup id="cite_ref-cind_24-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cind-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The passing trade gave rise to European, Chinese, Samoan, and other residents from the 1830s; they included <a href="/wiki/Beachcombing" title="Beachcombing">beachcombers</a>, castaways, traders, and missionaries.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Declaration_of_a_protectorate_on_Abemama_by_Captain_Davis,_27th_May_1892.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Declaration_of_a_protectorate_on_Abemama_by_Captain_Davis%2C_27th_May_1892.JPG/220px-Declaration_of_a_protectorate_on_Abemama_by_Captain_Davis%2C_27th_May_1892.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="136" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Declaration_of_a_protectorate_on_Abemama_by_Captain_Davis%2C_27th_May_1892.JPG/330px-Declaration_of_a_protectorate_on_Abemama_by_Captain_Davis%2C_27th_May_1892.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Declaration_of_a_protectorate_on_Abemama_by_Captain_Davis%2C_27th_May_1892.JPG/440px-Declaration_of_a_protectorate_on_Abemama_by_Captain_Davis%2C_27th_May_1892.JPG 2x" data-file-width="538" data-file-height="332" /></a><figcaption>Declaration of a protectorate on <a href="/wiki/Abemama" title="Abemama">Abemama</a> by Captain <a href="/wiki/Edward_H.M._Davis" class="mw-redirect" title="Edward H.M. Davis">EHM Davis</a>, 27 May 1892</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1886, an <a href="/wiki/Anglo-German_Declarations_about_the_Western_Pacific_Ocean" title="Anglo-German Declarations about the Western Pacific Ocean">Anglo-German agreement</a> partitioned the "unclaimed" central Pacific, leaving <a href="/wiki/Nauru" title="Nauru">Nauru</a> in the German sphere of influence, while <a href="/wiki/Banaba" title="Banaba">Ocean Island</a> and the future GEIC wound up in the <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British sphere of influence</a>. In 1892, local Gilbertese authorities (an <i>uea</i>, a chief from the Northern Gilbert Group, and <i>atun te boti</i> or head of clan<sup id="cite_ref-maude_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maude-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>) on each of the Gilbert Islands agreed to Captain <a href="/wiki/Edward_H.M._Davis" class="mw-redirect" title="Edward H.M. Davis">E.H.M. Davis</a> commanding <a href="/wiki/HMS_Royalist_(1883)" title="HMS Royalist (1883)">HMS <i>Royalist</i></a> of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a> declaring them part of a <a href="/wiki/British_protectorate" title="British protectorate">British protectorate</a>, along with the nearby <a href="/wiki/Ellice_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Ellice Islands">Ellice Islands</a>. They were administered by a <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_the_Gilbert_and_Ellice_Islands" title="Governor of the Gilbert and Ellice Islands">resident commissioner</a> based first on <a href="/wiki/Butaritari" title="Butaritari">Makin Islands</a> (1893–95), then in <a href="/wiki/Betio" title="Betio">Betio</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tarawa" title="Tarawa">Tarawa</a> (1896–1908) and <a href="/wiki/Banaba" title="Banaba">Ocean Island</a> (1908–1942), protectorate who was under the <a href="/wiki/High_Commissioner_for_the_Western_Pacific" title="High Commissioner for the Western Pacific">Western Pacific High Commission</a> based in Fiji.<sup id="cite_ref-BBCtimeline_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBCtimeline-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Banaba" title="Banaba">Banaba</a>, known to Europeans as Ocean Island, was added to the protectorate in 1900, because of the <a href="/wiki/Phosphorite" title="Phosphorite">phosphate rock</a> of its soil (discovered in 1900). This <a href="/wiki/Phosphate_mining_in_Banaba_and_Nauru" title="Phosphate mining in Banaba and Nauru">discovery and the mining</a> ended the contracting of <a href="/wiki/Kanaka_(Pacific_Island_worker)" title="Kanaka (Pacific Island worker)">Kanakas workers</a> to farm plantations in <a href="/wiki/Queensland" title="Queensland">Queensland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">German Samoa</a> or Central America, with all the needed workers being used in Ocean Island extraction.
</p><p>The conduct of <a href="/wiki/William_Telfer_Campbell" title="William Telfer Campbell">William Telfer Campbell</a>, the second resident commissioner of the Gilberts and Ellice Islands of 1896 to 1908, was criticised as to his legislative, judicial and administrative management (including allegations of forced labour exacted from islanders) and became the subject of the 1909 report by Arthur Mahaffy.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> In 1913, an anonymous correspondent to <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_Age" title="The New Age">The New Age</a></i> newspaper described the maladministration of W. Telfer Campbell and questioned the partiality of Arthur Mahaffy, because he was a former colonial official in the Gilberts.<sup id="cite_ref-AN_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AN-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> The anonymous correspondent also criticised the operations of the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Phosphate_Company" class="mw-redirect" title="Pacific Phosphate Company">Pacific Phosphate Company</a> on Ocean Island.<sup id="cite_ref-AN_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AN-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Boeing_314_Clipper_in_cruise.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Boeing_314_Clipper_in_cruise.jpg/220px-Boeing_314_Clipper_in_cruise.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Boeing_314_Clipper_in_cruise.jpg/330px-Boeing_314_Clipper_in_cruise.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Boeing_314_Clipper_in_cruise.jpg/440px-Boeing_314_Clipper_in_cruise.jpg 2x" data-file-width="550" data-file-height="310" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Boeing_314_Clipper" title="Boeing 314 Clipper">Boeing 314 Clipper</a> in cruise, 1940</figcaption></figure>
<p>The islands became the <a href="/wiki/Crown_colony" title="Crown colony">crown colony</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Gilbert_and_Ellice_Islands" title="Gilbert and Ellice Islands">Gilbert and Ellice Islands</a> in 1916.<sup id="cite_ref-brit_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The Northern Line Islands, including Christmas Island (<a href="/wiki/Kiritimati" title="Kiritimati">Kiritimati</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> were added to the colony in 1919, and the <a href="/wiki/Phoenix_Islands" title="Phoenix Islands">Phoenix Islands</a> were added in 1937 with the purpose of a <a href="/wiki/Phoenix_Islands_Settlement_Scheme" title="Phoenix Islands Settlement Scheme">Phoenix Islands Settlement Scheme</a>. On 12 July 1940, <a href="/wiki/Pan_Am" title="Pan Am">Pan Am</a> Airways' <i><a href="/wiki/Boeing_314_Clipper" title="Boeing 314 Clipper">American Clipper</a></i> landed at <a href="/wiki/Kanton_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Kanton Island">Canton Island</a> for the first time during a flight from <a href="/wiki/Honolulu" title="Honolulu">Honolulu</a> to <a href="/wiki/Auckland" title="Auckland">Auckland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sir <a href="/wiki/Arthur_Grimble" title="Arthur Grimble">Arthur Grimble</a> was a cadet administrative officer based at Tarawa (1913–1919) and became Resident Commissioner of the Gilbert and Ellice Islands colony in 1926.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGrimble1952_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrimble1952-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Stamp_Gilbert_Ellice_Islands_1939_3p.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Stamp_Gilbert_Ellice_Islands_1939_3p.jpg/220px-Stamp_Gilbert_Ellice_Islands_1939_3p.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Stamp_Gilbert_Ellice_Islands_1939_3p.jpg/330px-Stamp_Gilbert_Ellice_Islands_1939_3p.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Stamp_Gilbert_Ellice_Islands_1939_3p.jpg/440px-Stamp_Gilbert_Ellice_Islands_1939_3p.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="675" /></a><figcaption>Stamp with portrait of <a href="/wiki/George_VI" title="George VI">King George VI</a>, 1939</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1902, the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Cable_Station" title="Pacific Cable Station">Pacific Cable Board</a> laid the first trans-Pacific telegraph cable from <a href="/wiki/Bamfield" title="Bamfield">Bamfield</a>, British Columbia to <a href="/wiki/Fanning_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Fanning Island">Fanning Island</a> (Tabuaeran) in the Line Islands, and from Fiji to Fanning Island, thus completing the <a href="/wiki/All_Red_Line" title="All Red Line">All Red Line</a>, a series of telegraph lines circumnavigating the globe completely within the British Empire. The location of Fanning Island, one of the closest formations to Hawaii, led to its annexation by the British Empire in 1888. Nearby candidates including <a href="/wiki/Palmyra_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Palmyra Island">Palmyra Island</a> were not favoured due to the lack of adequate landing sites.
</p><p>The United States eventually incorporated the Northern Line Islands into its territories, and did the same with the <a href="/wiki/Phoenix_Islands" title="Phoenix Islands">Phoenix Islands</a>, which lie between Gilberts and the Line Islands, including <a href="/wiki/Howland_Island" title="Howland Island">Howland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jarvis_Island" title="Jarvis Island">Jarvis</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Baker_Island" title="Baker Island">Baker</a> islands, thus causing a territorial dispute. That was eventually resolved and they finally became part of Kiribati under the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Tarawa" title="Treaty of Tarawa">Treaty of Tarawa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tarawa.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Tarawa.jpg/220px-Tarawa.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="174" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Tarawa.jpg/330px-Tarawa.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Tarawa.jpg/440px-Tarawa.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2370" /></a><figcaption>American Marines assault a Japanese bunker during the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Tarawa" title="Battle of Tarawa">Battle of Tarawa</a>, November 1943.</figcaption></figure>
<p>After the <a href="/wiki/Attack_on_Pearl_Harbor" title="Attack on Pearl Harbor">attack on Pearl Harbor</a>, during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, <a href="/wiki/Butaritari" title="Butaritari">Butaritari</a> and Tarawa, and others of the Northern Gilbert group, were <a href="/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_the_Gilbert_Islands" title="Japanese occupation of the Gilbert Islands">occupied by Japan</a> from 1941 to 1943. <a href="/wiki/Betio" title="Betio">Betio</a> became an airfield and supply base. The expulsion of the Japanese military in late 1943 involved one of the bloodiest battles in <a href="/wiki/US_Marine_Corps" class="mw-redirect" title="US Marine Corps">US Marine Corps</a> history. Marines landed in November 1943 and the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Tarawa" title="Battle of Tarawa">Battle of Tarawa</a> ensued. <a href="/wiki/Banaba" title="Banaba">Ocean Island</a>, the headquarters of the colony, was bombed, evacuated and occupied by Japan in 1942 and only freed in 1945, after the massacre of all but one of the Gilbertese on the island by the Japanese forces. <a href="/wiki/Funafuti" title="Funafuti">Funafuti</a> hosted then the provisional headquarters of the colony from 1942 to 1946, when Tarawa returned to host the headquarters, replacing Ocean Island.
</p><p>At the end of 1945, most of the remaining inhabitants of Banaba, repatriated from <a href="/wiki/Kosrae" title="Kosrae">Kosrae</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nauru" title="Nauru">Nauru</a> and Tarawa, were relocated to <a href="/wiki/Rabi_Island" title="Rabi Island">Rabi Island</a>, a land of Fiji that the British government had acquired in 1942 for this purpose.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 1 January 1953, the <a href="/wiki/List_of_resident_commissioners_and_governors_of_the_Solomon_Islands" title="List of resident commissioners and governors of the Solomon Islands">British Western Pacific High Commissioner</a> of the colony was transferred from Fiji to the new capital of <a href="/wiki/Honiara" title="Honiara">Honiara</a>, to the <a href="/wiki/British_Solomon_Islands" title="British Solomon Islands">British Solomon Islands</a>, with the Gilberts' Resident Commissioner still headquartered in Tarawa.
</p><p>Further military operations in the colony occurred in the late 1950s and early 1960s when <a href="/wiki/Kiritimati" title="Kiritimati">Christmas Island</a> was used by the United States and United Kingdom for <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_testing" title="Nuclear weapons testing">nuclear weapons testing</a> including <a href="/wiki/Thermonuclear_weapon" title="Thermonuclear weapon">hydrogen bombs</a>.
</p><p>Institutions of internal self-rule were established on Tarawa from about 1967. The Ellice Islands asked for separation from the rest of the colony in 1974 and granted their own internal self-rule institutions. The separation entered into force on 1 January 1976. In 1978, the Ellice Islands became the independent nation of <a href="/wiki/Tuvalu" title="Tuvalu">Tuvalu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence">Independence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Independence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:KiribatiPresidential_Residence.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/KiribatiPresidential_Residence.jpg/220px-KiribatiPresidential_Residence.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/KiribatiPresidential_Residence.jpg/330px-KiribatiPresidential_Residence.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/KiribatiPresidential_Residence.jpg/440px-KiribatiPresidential_Residence.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="450" /></a><figcaption>The Presidential residence, former <a href="/wiki/Government_House" title="Government House">Government House</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bairiki" title="Bairiki">Bairiki</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The Gilbert Islands gained independence as the Republic of Kiribati on 12 July 1979.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Then, in September, the United States relinquished all claims to the sparsely inhabited <a href="/wiki/Phoenix_Islands" title="Phoenix Islands">Phoenix</a> and Line Islands, in a 1979 treaty of friendship with Kiribati (<a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Tarawa" title="Treaty of Tarawa">ratified in 1983</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Although the indigenous <a href="/wiki/Gilbertese_language" title="Gilbertese language">Gilbertese</a> name for the Gilbert Islands proper is "Tungaru", the new state chose the name "Kiribati", the Gilbertese spelling of "Gilberts", because it was more modern and as an equivalent of the former colony to acknowledge the inclusion of Banaba, the Line Islands, and the <a href="/wiki/Phoenix_Islands" title="Phoenix Islands">Phoenix Islands</a>. The last two archipelagoes were never initially occupied by Gilbertese until the British authorities, and later the Republic Government, resettled Gilbertese there under resettlement schemes.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_27-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> In 1982, the first <a href="/wiki/1982_Kiribati_parliamentary_election" title="1982 Kiribati parliamentary election">elections since independence</a> were held. A no-confidence vote provoked the <a href="/wiki/1983_Kiribati_parliamentary_election" title="1983 Kiribati parliamentary election">1983 new election</a>. In the post-independence era, <a href="/wiki/Overcrowding" title="Overcrowding">overcrowding</a> has been an issue, at least in British and aid organisations' eyes. In 1988, an announcement was made that 4,700 residents of the main island group would be resettled onto less populated islands. In September 1994, <a href="/wiki/Teburoro_Tito" title="Teburoro Tito">Teburoro Tito</a> from the opposition was elected president.
</p><p>In 1995, Kiribati unilaterally moved the <a href="/wiki/International_date_line" class="mw-redirect" title="International date line">international date line</a> far to the east to encompass the Line Islands group, so that the nation would no longer be divided by the date line. The move, which fulfilled one of President Tito's campaign promises, was intended to allow businesses across the expansive nation to keep the same business week. This also enabled Kiribati to become the first country to see the dawn of the <a href="/wiki/Third_millennium" class="mw-redirect" title="Third millennium">third millennium</a>, an event of significance for tourism. Tito was re-elected in 1998. In 1999, Kiribati became a full member of the United Nations, 20 years after independence.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> In 2002, Kiribati passed a controversial law that enabled the government to shut down newspaper publishers. The legislation followed the launching of Kiribati's first successful non-government-run newspaper. President Tito was re-elected in 2003 but was removed from office in March 2003 by a no-confidence vote and replaced by a Council of State. <a href="/wiki/Anote_Tong" title="Anote Tong">Anote Tong</a> of the opposition party <a href="/wiki/Boutokaan_Te_Koaua" class="mw-redirect" title="Boutokaan Te Koaua">Boutokaan Te Koaua</a> was elected to succeed Tito in July 2003. He was re-elected in 2007 and in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In June 2008, Kiribati officials asked Australia and New Zealand to accept Kiribati citizens as permanent refugees. Kiribati is expected to be the first country to lose all its land territory to <a href="/wiki/Climate_change" title="Climate change">climate change</a>. In June 2008, Kiribati President Anote Tong said that the country had reached "the point of no return." He added, "To plan for the day when you no longer have a country is indeed painful but I think we have to do that."<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In January 2012, Anote Tong was re-elected for a third and last successive term. In early 2012, the government of Kiribati purchased the 2,200-hectare Natoavatu Estate on the second largest island of Fiji, <a href="/wiki/Vanua_Levu" title="Vanua Levu">Vanua Levu</a>. At the time it was widely reported<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> that the government planned to evacuate the entire population of Kiribati to Fiji. In April 2013, President Tong began urging citizens to evacuate the islands and migrate elsewhere.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> In May 2014, the Office of the President confirmed the purchase of some 5,460 acres of land on Vanua Levu at a cost of 9.3&#160;million Australian dollars.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 2016, <a href="/wiki/Taneti_Maamau" title="Taneti Maamau">Taneti Maamau</a> was <a href="/wiki/2016_Kiribati_presidential_election" title="2016 Kiribati presidential election">elected</a> as the new President of Kiribati. He was the fifth president since the country became independent in 1979.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> In June 2020, President Maamau won <a href="/wiki/2020_Kiribati_presidential_election" title="2020 Kiribati presidential election">re-election</a> for a second four-year term. President Maamau was considered pro-China and he supported closer ties with Beijing.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> On 16 November 2021, the Kiribati government announced it would expose the world's largest marine protected area to commercial fishing. The <a href="/wiki/2022_Kiribati_constitutional_crisis" title="2022 Kiribati constitutional crisis">2022 Kiribati constitutional crisis</a> started with the suspension of all 5 major Justices of the <a href="/wiki/Judiciary_of_Kiribati" title="Judiciary of Kiribati">judiciary of Kiribati</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2020, <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Kiribati" title="COVID-19 pandemic in Kiribati">Kiribati’s response to the COVID-19 pandemic</a>, consistent with most of the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Oceania" title="COVID-19 pandemic in Oceania"> COVID-19 responses of Oceania island nations</a>, was to impose strict limits on tourism and commercial travel. Kiribati reported that it remained essentially COVID-free (two cases) until January 2022 when the first commercial international flight in two years included 36 passengers who tested positive. While new restrictions were imposed, Kiribati subsequently reported a total of 3252 cases and 13 deaths.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Kiribati" title="Politics of Kiribati">Politics of Kiribati</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:KiribatiParliamentHouse.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/KiribatiParliamentHouse.jpg/220px-KiribatiParliamentHouse.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/KiribatiParliamentHouse.jpg/330px-KiribatiParliamentHouse.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/KiribatiParliamentHouse.jpg/440px-KiribatiParliamentHouse.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="450" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Maneaba" title="Maneaba">Maneaba</a> ni Maungatabu, House of Assembly, 2000.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Kiribati" title="Constitution of Kiribati">Constitution of Kiribati</a>, promulgated 12 July 1979, provides for free and open elections in a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">democratic</a> republic.
</p><p>The executive branch consists of a <a href="/wiki/President_of_Kiribati" title="President of Kiribati">president</a> (<i>te Beretitenti</i>), a <a href="/wiki/Vice-President_of_Kiribati" title="Vice-President of Kiribati">vice-president</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Cabinet_of_Kiribati" title="Cabinet of Kiribati">cabinet</a>. The president, who is also chief of the cabinet, is directly elected by the citizens, after the legislature nominates three or four persons from among its members to be candidates in the ensuing presidential election. The president is limited to serving three four-year terms, and remains a member of the assembly. The cabinet is composed of the president, vice-president, and 13 ministers (appointed by the president) who are also <a href="/wiki/House_of_Assembly_(Kiribati)" title="House of Assembly (Kiribati)">ministers of parliament</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-US_State_Dept_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_State_Dept-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The legislative branch is the unicameral <i><a href="/wiki/House_of_Assembly_(Kiribati)" title="House of Assembly (Kiribati)">Maneaba ni Maungatabu</a></i> (House of Assembly). Its members are elected, including by constitutional mandate, a nominated representative of the <a href="/wiki/Banaban_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Banaban people">Banaban people</a> in <a href="/wiki/Rabi_Island" title="Rabi Island">Rabi Island</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a> (Banaba, former Ocean Island), in addition to, until 2016, the attorney general, who served as an <i>ex officio</i> member from 1979 to 2016. Legislators serve for a four-year term.
</p><p>The constitutional provisions governing administration of justice are similar to those in other former British possessions in that the judiciary is free from governmental interference. The judicial branch is made up of the High Court (in Betio) and the Court of Appeal.<sup id="cite_ref-US_State_Dept_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_State_Dept-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The president appoints the presiding judges.
</p><p>Local government is through island councils with elected members. Local affairs are handled in a manner similar to town meetings in colonial America. Island councils make their own estimates of revenue and expenditure<sup id="cite_ref-US_State_Dept_64-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_State_Dept-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> and generally are free from central government controls. There are a total of 21 inhabited islands in Kiribati. Each inhabited island has its own council. Since independence, Kiribati is no longer divided into districts (see <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Kiribati" class="mw-redirect" title="Districts of Kiribati">Subdivisions of Kiribati</a>).
</p><p>Kiribati has formal political parties but their organisation is quite informal.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Ad&#160;hoc opposition groups tend to coalesce around specific issues. There is universal suffrage at age 18.<sup id="cite_ref-US_State_Dept_64-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_State_Dept-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Today the only recognisable parties are the <a href="/wiki/Boutokaan_Kiribati_Moa_Party" title="Boutokaan Kiribati Moa Party">Boutokaan Kiribati Moa Party</a>, former <i>Boutokaan te Koaua</i>, and <a href="/wiki/Tobwaan_Kiribati_Party" title="Tobwaan Kiribati Party">Tobwaan Kiribati Party</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Kiribati" title="Foreign relations of Kiribati">Foreign relations of Kiribati</a></div>
<p>Kiribati maintains close relations with its Pacific neighbours, Australia, New Zealand, Japan and Fiji. The first three of these provide the bulk of the country's foreign aid. <a href="/wiki/Taiwan" title="Taiwan">Taiwan</a> and Japan also have specified-period licences to fish in Kiribati's waters.<sup id="cite_ref-US_State_Dept_64-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_State_Dept-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> There are three resident diplomatic missions headquartered in <a href="/wiki/South_Tarawa" title="South Tarawa">South Tarawa</a>: the Embassies of the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_China" class="mw-redirect" title="Republic of China">Republic of China</a> (Taiwan) until 2019, replaced by <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">People's Republic of China</a> in 2020 and the High Commissions of <a href="/wiki/List_of_Australian_High_Commissioners_to_Kiribati" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Australian High Commissioners to Kiribati">Australia</a> and <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a>. A <a href="/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_the_United_States" title="List of diplomatic missions of the United States">US Embassy</a> will open in 2023.
</p><p>In November 1999, Kiribati agreed to allow Japan's <a href="/wiki/National_Space_Development_Agency" class="mw-redirect" title="National Space Development Agency">National Space Development Agency</a> to lease land on <a href="/wiki/Kiritimati" title="Kiritimati">Kiritimati</a> (formerly Christmas Island) for 20 years, on which to build a <a href="/wiki/Spaceport" title="Spaceport">spaceport</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-SpaceDaily2000_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SpaceDaily2000-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> The agreement stipulated that Japan was to pay US$840,000 per year and would also pay for any damage to roads and the environment.<sup id="cite_ref-SpaceDaily2000_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SpaceDaily2000-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> A Japanese-built downrange <a href="/wiki/Tracking_station" class="mw-redirect" title="Tracking station">tracking station</a> operates on Kiritimati<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> and an abandoned airfield on the island was designated as the landing strip for a proposed reusable unmanned space shuttle called <a href="/wiki/HOPE-X" title="HOPE-X">HOPE-X</a>. HOPE-X, however, was eventually cancelled by Japan in 2003.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:%E4%BA%94%E6%9C%88%E4%BA%8C%E5%8D%81%E4%B8%89%E6%97%A5%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E6%8E%A5%E8%A6%8B%E5%90%89%E9%87%8C%E5%B7%B4%E6%96%AF%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E9%A6%AC%E8%8C%82(Taneti_Maamau)%E4%BC%89%E5%84%B7%E4%B9%99%E8%A1%8C_(27208262585).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/%E4%BA%94%E6%9C%88%E4%BA%8C%E5%8D%81%E4%B8%89%E6%97%A5%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E6%8E%A5%E8%A6%8B%E5%90%89%E9%87%8C%E5%B7%B4%E6%96%AF%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E9%A6%AC%E8%8C%82%28Taneti_Maamau%29%E4%BC%89%E5%84%B7%E4%B9%99%E8%A1%8C_%2827208262585%29.jpg/220px-%E4%BA%94%E6%9C%88%E4%BA%8C%E5%8D%81%E4%B8%89%E6%97%A5%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E6%8E%A5%E8%A6%8B%E5%90%89%E9%87%8C%E5%B7%B4%E6%96%AF%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E9%A6%AC%E8%8C%82%28Taneti_Maamau%29%E4%BC%89%E5%84%B7%E4%B9%99%E8%A1%8C_%2827208262585%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/%E4%BA%94%E6%9C%88%E4%BA%8C%E5%8D%81%E4%B8%89%E6%97%A5%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E6%8E%A5%E8%A6%8B%E5%90%89%E9%87%8C%E5%B7%B4%E6%96%AF%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E9%A6%AC%E8%8C%82%28Taneti_Maamau%29%E4%BC%89%E5%84%B7%E4%B9%99%E8%A1%8C_%2827208262585%29.jpg/330px-%E4%BA%94%E6%9C%88%E4%BA%8C%E5%8D%81%E4%B8%89%E6%97%A5%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E6%8E%A5%E8%A6%8B%E5%90%89%E9%87%8C%E5%B7%B4%E6%96%AF%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E9%A6%AC%E8%8C%82%28Taneti_Maamau%29%E4%BC%89%E5%84%B7%E4%B9%99%E8%A1%8C_%2827208262585%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/%E4%BA%94%E6%9C%88%E4%BA%8C%E5%8D%81%E4%B8%89%E6%97%A5%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E6%8E%A5%E8%A6%8B%E5%90%89%E9%87%8C%E5%B7%B4%E6%96%AF%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E9%A6%AC%E8%8C%82%28Taneti_Maamau%29%E4%BC%89%E5%84%B7%E4%B9%99%E8%A1%8C_%2827208262585%29.jpg/440px-%E4%BA%94%E6%9C%88%E4%BA%8C%E5%8D%81%E4%B8%89%E6%97%A5%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E6%8E%A5%E8%A6%8B%E5%90%89%E9%87%8C%E5%B7%B4%E6%96%AF%E7%B8%BD%E7%B5%B1%E9%A6%AC%E8%8C%82%28Taneti_Maamau%29%E4%BC%89%E5%84%B7%E4%B9%99%E8%A1%8C_%2827208262585%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4517" data-file-height="3011" /></a><figcaption>Kiribati President <a href="/wiki/Taneti_Maamau" title="Taneti Maamau">Taneti Maamau</a> meets with Taiwanese President <a href="/wiki/Tsai_Ing-wen" title="Tsai Ing-wen">Tsai Ing-wen</a> on 23 May 2016</figcaption></figure>
<p>As one of the world's most vulnerable nations to the <a href="/wiki/Effects_of_global_warming" class="mw-redirect" title="Effects of global warming">effects of global warming</a>, Kiribati has been an active participant in international diplomatic efforts relating to climate change, most importantly the <a href="/wiki/UNFCCC" class="mw-redirect" title="UNFCCC">UNFCCC</a> conferences of the parties (COP). Kiribati is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Alliance_of_Small_Island_States" title="Alliance of Small Island States">Alliance of Small Island States</a> (AOSIS), an intergovernmental organisation of low-lying coastal and small island countries.
Established in 1990, the main purpose of the alliance is to consolidate the voices of <a href="/wiki/Small_Island_Developing_States" title="Small Island Developing States">Small Island Developing States</a> (SIDS) to address global warming. AOSIS has been very active from its inception, putting forward the first draft text in the <a href="/wiki/Kyoto_Protocol" title="Kyoto Protocol">Kyoto Protocol</a> negotiations as early as 1994.
</p><p>In 2009, President Tong attended the <a href="/wiki/Climate_Vulnerable_Forum" title="Climate Vulnerable Forum">Climate Vulnerable Forum</a> (<a href="/wiki/Climate_Vulnerable_Forum" title="Climate Vulnerable Forum">V11</a>) in the <a href="/wiki/Maldives" title="Maldives">Maldives</a>, with 10 other countries that are <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_vulnerability" title="Climate change vulnerability">vulnerable to climate change</a>, and signed the Bandos Island declaration on 10 November 2009, pledging to show moral leadership and commence greening their economies by voluntarily committing to achieving <a href="/wiki/Carbon_neutrality" class="mw-redirect" title="Carbon neutrality">carbon neutrality</a>.
</p><p>In November 2010, Kiribati hosted the <a href="/wiki/Tarawa_Climate_Change_Conference" title="Tarawa Climate Change Conference">Tarawa Climate Change Conference</a> (TCCC) to support the president of Kiribati's initiative to hold a consultative forum between vulnerable states and their partners. The conference strove to create an enabling environment for multi-party negotiations under the auspices of the UNFCCC. The conference was a successor event to the Climate Vulnerable Forum.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> The ultimate objective of TCCC was to reduce the number and intensity of fault lines between parties to the COP process, explore elements of agreement between the parties and thereby to support Kiribati's and other parties' contribution to <a href="/wiki/COP16" class="mw-redirect" title="COP16">COP16</a> held in <a href="/wiki/Cancun" class="mw-redirect" title="Cancun">Cancun</a>, Mexico, from 29 November to 10 December 2010.
</p><p>In 2013, President Tong spoke of climate-change induced <a href="/wiki/Sea_level_rise" title="Sea level rise">sea level rise</a> as "inevitable". "For our people to survive, then they will have to migrate. Either we can wait for the time when we have to move people en masse or we can prepare them—beginning from now ..."<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> In New York in 2014, per <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_Yorker" title="The New Yorker">The New Yorker</a></i>, President Tong told <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times">The New York Times</a></i> that "according to the projections, within this century, the water will be higher than the highest point in our lands".<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> In 2014, President Tong finalised the purchase of a 20&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (7.7&#160;sq&#160;mi) stretch of land on <a href="/wiki/Vanua_Levu" title="Vanua Levu">Vanua Levu</a>, one of the larger Fiji islands, 2,000&#160;km away. A move described by Tong as an "absolute necessity" should the nation be completely submerged under water.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2013, attention was drawn to a claim of a Kiribati man of being a <a href="/wiki/Environmental_migrant#Asia_and_the_Pacific" title="Environmental migrant">"climate change refugee"</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Convention_relating_to_the_Status_of_Refugees" class="mw-redirect" title="Convention relating to the Status of Refugees">Convention relating to the Status of Refugees</a> (1951).<sup id="cite_ref-ABC_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ABC-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> However, this claim was determined by the New Zealand High Court to be untenable.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> The New Zealand Court of Appeal also rejected the claim in a <a href="/wiki/Teitiota_v_Chief_Executive_Ministry_of_Business,_Innovation_and_Employment" title="Teitiota v Chief Executive Ministry of Business, Innovation and Employment">2014 decision</a>.
On further appeal, the New Zealand Supreme Court confirmed the earlier adverse rulings against the application for refugee status, but rejected the proposition "that environmental degradation resulting from climate change or other natural disasters could never create a pathway into the Refugee Convention or protected person jurisdiction".<sup id="cite_ref-TSC_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TSC-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> In 2017, Kiribati signed the UN <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons" title="Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons">treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 20 September 2019, the government of Kiribati restored its diplomatic relationship with the People's Republic of China and simultaneously stopped its diplomatic relationship with Taiwan.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> China offered a 737 aircraft and ferries to Kiribati for the decision, according to Taiwan's foreign minister, <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Wu" title="Joseph Wu">Joseph Wu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Peace_Corps">Peace Corps</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Peace Corps">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>From 1973 though 2008, a total of almost 500 <a href="/wiki/Peace_Corps" title="Peace Corps">US Peace Corps</a> volunteers were based on the Islands, as many as 45 in a given year. Activities included assisting in the planning, design and construction of wells, libraries, and other infrastructure, and agricultural, environmental, and community health education. In 2006, volunteer placement was significantly scaled down due to the reduction of consistent air transportation to the outer islands; it was later ended because the associated ability to provide medical care to volunteers could not be assured.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>  In July 2022, US Vice President Harris announced plans to build a new embassy in Kiribati and Tonga and reestablish the Peace Corps presence in the region.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Law_enforcement_and_military">Law enforcement and military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Law enforcement and military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:RKS_Teanoai,_during_a_joint_exercise_2019-11-07.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/RKS_Teanoai%2C_during_a_joint_exercise_2019-11-07.jpg/220px-RKS_Teanoai%2C_during_a_joint_exercise_2019-11-07.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/RKS_Teanoai%2C_during_a_joint_exercise_2019-11-07.jpg/330px-RKS_Teanoai%2C_during_a_joint_exercise_2019-11-07.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/8e/RKS_Teanoai%2C_during_a_joint_exercise_2019-11-07.jpg 2x" data-file-width="359" data-file-height="307" /></a><figcaption>Police vessel RKS <i>Teanoai</i>, in 2019.</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_Kiribati" title="Law enforcement in Kiribati">Law enforcement in Kiribati</a> is carried out by the Kiribati Police Service which is responsible for all law enforcement and paramilitary duties for the island nation. There are police posts located on all of the islands. The police have one patrol boat, the <a href="/wiki/Pacific-class" class="mw-redirect" title="Pacific-class">Pacific-class</a> patrol boat <a href="/wiki/RKS_Teanoai_II_(301)" class="mw-redirect" title="RKS Teanoai II (301)">RKS <i>Teanoai II</i></a>.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> Kiribati has no military and relies on both Australia and New Zealand for its defence.
</p><p>The main prison in Kiribati is located in <a href="/wiki/Betio" title="Betio">Betio</a>, named the Walter Betio Prison. There is also a prison in <a href="/wiki/London,_Kiribati" title="London, Kiribati">London</a> on <a href="/wiki/Kiritimati" title="Kiritimati">Kiritimati</a>.
</p><p>Male <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Kiribati" title="LGBT rights in Kiribati">homosexuality</a> is illegal in Kiribati, with a penalty up to 14 years in prison, according to a historical British law, but this law is not enforced. Kiribati has not yet followed the lead of the United Kingdom, following its <a href="/wiki/Wolfenden_report" title="Wolfenden report">Wolfenden report</a>, to decriminalise acts of male homosexuality, beginning with provisions in the UK's Sexual Offences Act 1957. Female homosexuality is legal, but lesbians may face violence and discrimination. However, employment discrimination on the basis of sexual orientation is prohibited.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:CarolinePic-Kepler-Long.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/CarolinePic-Kepler-Long.JPG/220px-CarolinePic-Kepler-Long.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/CarolinePic-Kepler-Long.JPG/330px-CarolinePic-Kepler-Long.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/CarolinePic-Kepler-Long.JPG/440px-CarolinePic-Kepler-Long.JPG 2x" data-file-width="495" data-file-height="331" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Line_Islands" title="Line Islands">Line Islands</a>: <a href="/wiki/Millenium_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Millenium Island">Millenium Island</a> channel between west side of Long Island and Nake Island.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Marakei_Atoll.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Marakei_Atoll.jpg/220px-Marakei_Atoll.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Marakei_Atoll.jpg/330px-Marakei_Atoll.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Marakei_Atoll.jpg/440px-Marakei_Atoll.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3060" data-file-height="2033" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Marakei" title="Marakei">Marakei</a>, North Gilbert Islands</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Kiribati" class="mw-redirect" title="Districts of Kiribati">Districts of Kiribati</a></div>
<p>There are 21 inhabited islands in Kiribati. Kiribati can be geographically divided into three <a href="/wiki/Archipelago" title="Archipelago">archipelagoes</a> or groups of islands, which have no administrative functions. They are:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Gilbert_Islands" title="Gilbert Islands">Gilbert Islands</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Phoenix_Islands" title="Phoenix Islands">Phoenix Islands</a>, in one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_largest_protected_areas_in_the_world" class="mw-redirect" title="List of largest protected areas in the world">largest marine protected areas</a> on Earth (was the largest from 2008 to 2010)<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Line_Islands" title="Line Islands">Line Islands</a></li></ul>
<p>The original districts before independence were:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Banaba" title="Banaba">Banaba</a> (Ocean Island)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Tarawa_Atoll" class="mw-redirect" title="Tarawa Atoll">Tarawa Atoll</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Northern_Gilbert_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Gilbert Islands">Northern Gilbert Islands</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Central_Gilbert_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Central Gilbert Island">Central Gilbert Island</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/South_Gilbert_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="South Gilbert Islands">Southern Gilbert Islands</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Line_Islands" title="Line Islands">Line Islands</a></li></ul>
<p>Four of the former districts (including Tarawa) lie in the Gilbert Islands, where most of the country's population lives. Five of the Line Islands are uninhabited (<a href="/wiki/Malden_Island" title="Malden Island">Malden Island</a>, <a href="/wiki/Starbuck_Island" title="Starbuck Island">Starbuck Island</a>, <a href="/wiki/Millenium_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Millenium Island">Millenium Island</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vostok_Island" title="Vostok Island">Vostok Island</a> and <a href="/wiki/Flint_Island" title="Flint Island">Flint Island</a>). The Phoenix Islands are uninhabited except for <a href="/wiki/Kanton_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Kanton Island">Kanton</a>, and have no representation. Banaba itself is sparsely inhabited now. There is also a non-elected representative of the Banabans on <a href="/wiki/Rabi_Island" title="Rabi Island">Rabi Island</a> in Fiji.
</p><p>Each of the 21 inhabited islands<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> has its own local council that takes care of daily affairs. There is one council for each inhabited island, with two exceptions: Tarawa Atoll has three councils: <a href="/wiki/Betio" title="Betio">Betio</a> Town Council, <a href="/wiki/Teinainano_Urban_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="Teinainano Urban Council">Teinainano Urban Council</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teinainano_Urban_Council" class="extiw" title="it:Teinainano Urban Council">it</a>&#93;</span> (TUC) (for the rest of <a href="/wiki/South_Tarawa" title="South Tarawa">South Tarawa</a>) and Eutan Tarawa Council (ETC) (for <a href="/wiki/North_Tarawa" title="North Tarawa">North Tarawa</a>); and <a href="/wiki/Tabiteuea" title="Tabiteuea">Tabiteuea</a> has two councils.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Kiribati" title="Geography of Kiribati">Geography of Kiribati</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_of_the_Territorial_Waters_of_the_Pacific_Ocean.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Map_of_the_Territorial_Waters_of_the_Pacific_Ocean.png/510px-Map_of_the_Territorial_Waters_of_the_Pacific_Ocean.png" decoding="async" width="510" height="279" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Map_of_the_Territorial_Waters_of_the_Pacific_Ocean.png/765px-Map_of_the_Territorial_Waters_of_the_Pacific_Ocean.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Map_of_the_Territorial_Waters_of_the_Pacific_Ocean.png/1020px-Map_of_the_Territorial_Waters_of_the_Pacific_Ocean.png 2x" data-file-width="3067" data-file-height="1678" /></a><figcaption>Kiribati with its surrounding EEZs in dark gray, noting the three non-contiguous territories.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kiribati_map_LOC.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Kiribati_map_LOC.jpg/220px-Kiribati_map_LOC.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="178" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Kiribati_map_LOC.jpg/330px-Kiribati_map_LOC.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Kiribati_map_LOC.jpg/440px-Kiribati_map_LOC.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2399" data-file-height="1943" /></a><figcaption>Map of Kiribati.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Abaiang_top_view.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Abaiang_top_view.jpg/220px-Abaiang_top_view.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Abaiang_top_view.jpg/330px-Abaiang_top_view.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Abaiang_top_view.jpg/440px-Abaiang_top_view.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1400" data-file-height="1050" /></a><figcaption>Coconut palms in Abaiang</figcaption></figure>
<p>Kiribati consists of <a href="/wiki/List_of_islands_of_Kiribati" title="List of islands of Kiribati">32</a> atolls and one solitary island (Banaba), extending into the eastern and western <a href="/wiki/Hemispheres_of_Earth" title="Hemispheres of Earth">hemispheres</a>, as well as the northern and southern hemispheres. Its extensive <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> covers three, non-contiguous, traditional geographic subregions: Banaba (<a href="/wiki/Melanesia" title="Melanesia">Melanesian</a>-Micronesian area), the Gilbert Islands (Micronesia) and the <a href="/wiki/Line_Islands" title="Line Islands">Line</a> and Phoenix Islands (<a href="/wiki/Polynesia" title="Polynesia">Polynesia</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The groups of islands are:
</p>
<ul><li>Banaba: an isolated island between <a href="/wiki/Nauru" title="Nauru">Nauru</a> and the Gilbert Islands</li>
<li>Gilbert Islands: 16 atolls located some 1,500 kilometres (932&#160;mi) north of <a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a></li>
<li>Phoenix Islands: 8 atolls and coral islands located some 1,800 kilometres (1,118&#160;mi) southeast of the Gilberts</li>
<li>Line Islands: 8 atolls and one reef, located about 3,300 kilometres (2,051&#160;mi) east of the Gilberts</li></ul>
<p>Banaba (or Ocean Island) is a <a href="/wiki/Raised_coral_atoll" title="Raised coral atoll">raised-coral island</a>. It was once a rich source of <a href="/wiki/Phosphate" title="Phosphate">phosphates</a>, but was exhausted in mining before independence.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliamsMacdonald1985_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliamsMacdonald1985-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEllis1935_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEllis1935-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The rest of the land in Kiribati consists of the sand and reef rock islets of atolls or coral islands, which rise only one or two metres above sea level.
</p><p>The soil is thin and <a href="/wiki/Calcareous" title="Calcareous">calcareous</a>. It has a low water-holding capacity and low organic matter and nutrient content—except for calcium, sodium, and magnesium. Banaba is one of the least suitable places for agriculture in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThomas20033_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas20033-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Kiritimati" title="Kiritimati">Kiritimati</a> (previously Christmas Island) in the Line Islands is the world's largest atoll. Based on a 1995 realignment of the <a href="/wiki/International_Date_Line" title="International Date Line">International Date Line</a>, the Line Islands were the first area to enter into a new year, including year 2000. For that reason, Caroline Island was renamed <a href="/wiki/Millennium_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Millennium Island">Millennium Island</a> in 1997.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environmental_issues">Environmental issues</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kiribati&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Environmental issues">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Coral_reefs_of_Kiribati" title="Coral reefs of Kiribati">Coral reefs of Kiribati</a></div>
<p>According to the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Regional_Environment_Programme" title="Pacific Regional Environment Programme">Pacific Regional Environment Programme</a> (previously South Pacific Regional Environment Programme), two small uninhabited Kiribati islets, <a href="/wiki/Tebua_Tarawa" title="Tebua Tarawa">Tebua Tarawa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Abanuea" title="Abanuea">Abanuea</a>, disappeared underwater in 1999.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> The United Nations <a href="/wiki/Intergovernmental_Panel_on_Climate_Change" title="Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change">Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change</a> predicts that sea levels will rise by about 50&#160;cm (20&#160;in) by 2100 due to <a href="/wiki/Global_warming" class="mw-redirect" title="Global warming">global warming</a> and a further rise would be inevitable. It is thus likely that within a century the nation's arable land will become subject to increased <a href="/wiki/Soil_salination" class="mw-redirect" title="Soil salination">soil salination</a> and will be largely submerged.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
